### Pandulphus <note>Pandulphus is a recurring figure in alchemical literature, often associated with the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts.</note>
You seekers of wisdom should all know together / that this abiding water is a pure **Water of Life** / and that no **poison** <note>original: *Gifft*; in this context, it refers to a potent, transformative medicinal substance rather than a toxin.</note> that **dyes** <note>original: *tingiret*; to "tincture" or change the color and nature of a metal.</note> is made without the **Sun** and its shadow : for whoever has dyed the poison of the sages with the Sun and its shadow / has arrived at a great mystery.

### Exercise 9 <note>original: *Exercitatio 9*</note>
Divide the elements by fire / and with the help of **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurii*</note> put them back together / which is the greatest secret / and thus our **Masterpiece** <term>Masterpiece|original: *Magisterium*; the completed alchemical work or the Philosopher's Stone itself.</term> is fulfilled etc.

### Exercise 14 <note>original: *Exercitatio 14*</note>
Among these three, one always helps the other ; for the **Spirit** <note>original: *Spiritus*</note> preserves the **Body** <note>original: *Cörper*</note> / so that it is not burned by the fire : and the clear Body preserves the Spirit / so that it does not flee from the fire : for the Body is **fixed** <term>fixed|original: *fix*; a substance that is stable in heat and does not evaporate or "fly" away.</term> / therefore it holds the Spirit so that it does not escape : and the Spirit is incombustible / therefore it does not let the Body burn : for the Spirit and Body are one / by means of the **Soul** <note>original: *Seele*</note> / which is present with the spirit and body. But if the Soul were not there / then the spirit and body would be separated from each other by the fire : But when the Soul is joined to the spirit and body / then the whole work cares nothing for the fire / or for any thing of this world.

### Rhasis <note>Referring to Al-Razi (c. 865–925 AD), a Persian physician and alchemist whose works were fundamental to medieval science.</note> in the *Book of Lights* <note>original: *libro luminum*</note> :
Our **Stone** has its name from the creation of the world / which is threefold and one.

--- Page 170 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of the alchemical "Masterpiece," specifically focusing on the purification of Mercury and the necessity of achieving a "subtle" or ethereal state of matter through the dissolution of Body and Spirit.</meta>

->❦(156)❧<-

Those same **philosophers** <note>original: *Philosophi*</note> have yielded unequal things: but in no bodies is **Mercury** <term>Mercury|original: *Mercurius*</term> found purer or more perfect than in these three: namely in gold, silver, and common quicksilver.

When body and spirit are dissolved, then a general **dissolution** <term>dissolution|original: *Solutio*</term> occurs, because both create the four **elements** <term>elements|original: *Elementa*</term> and bring forth a firm and fixed substance: But if they are not both dissolved, it results only in a close relationship and a portion of the mixture which the fire can still separate.

<margin>Rosinus to Saratant <note>Rosinus is the Latinized name of Zosimos of Panopolis; "Saratant" refers to a dialogue partner in these hermetic texts.</note></margin> In our **Masterpiece** <term>Masterpiece|original: *Magisterium*</term> there is a **spirit** and **bodies** <note>original: *Spiritus und Corpora*</note>, whence the verse: It rejoices over the roasted seed, when the three are united together.

<margin>Khalid: <note>Referring to Khalid ibn Yazid, an early Umayyad prince associated with the beginnings of alchemy.</note></margin> Prepare the strong bodies with the purified moisture, until each of them is ennobled to its **subtlety** <term>subtlety|original: *Subtielheit*; a state of matter so fine and purified that it takes on spiritual properties.</term>.

Likewise, there is also among them a saying of a wise man: If you do not transform the bodies to a **subtlety**, so that they are thin and cannot be felt when touched, then that which you seek will not proceed correctly for you; and if they are not ground down, then turn back to the work, etc.

<margin>Rosinus:</margin> But our **Stone** <note>original: *Stein*; the Philosopher's Stone</note> is a body, **soul** <note>original: *S[eele]*; the text is truncated at the margin.</note>, and spirit or water, as the philosophers say, and is made in one vessel, and they have spoken rightly, for our entire Masterpiece...

<summary>This page details the purification of Mercury through gold, silver, and quicksilver, emphasizing that the Alchemical Stone must be ground and dissolved into a "subtle" state where physical matter and spirit become indistinguishable.</summary>
<keywords>Mercury, gold, silver, dissolution, four elements, subtlety, Rosinus, Khalid, Masterpiece, Philosopher's Stone</keywords>

The catchword "Pul-" suggests the next page begins with a discussion of "Pulver" (powder).</meta>

(157)

with our water and made from the same. For this dissolves the bodies, as has been said, not with such a **dissolution** <term>dissolution|original: *solution*; the process of breaking down a solid into a liquid state.</term> as some believe—thinking it is transformed into water from the clouds <note>The author is clarifying that this is not literal rainwater, but a specialized alchemical solvent.</note>—but through a **philosophical dissolution**, so that it is a water from which the metals are formed; and when this is now **calcined** <term>calcined|original: *calcinirt*; the process of heating a substance to high temperatures to reduce it to a dry powder or ash.</term> and turned into earth, it brings those same bodies into whatever thing it is brought, into its substance; it makes them yellow like wax, makes them white, and cleanses the **laton** <term>laton|original: *latonem*; a term for an impure metal or alloy, often brass or copper, that must be "whitened" or purified in the alchemical process.</term> according to the word of **Morienus** <note>referring to Morienus Romanus, a legendary hermit and alchemist of the 7th century.</note>.

In the small treatise of **Aristotle** <note>referring to the *De Perfecto Magisterio* or other pseudo-Aristotelian alchemical works common in the Middle Ages and Renaissance.</note>: therefore take your dearest son and place him in equality with his dearest sister; bring to them a cup of love, for the unity of a willing inclination joins one thing to the other.

It follows, however: But yet you should know that everything that is pure must agree with the pure, otherwise they beget children who are not like them. And therefore, as **Avicenna** <note>The Latinized name of Ibn Sina, the Persian polymath whose name was frequently attached to alchemical manuscripts.</note> says, the first thing you must do in this work is to **sublimate** the **Mercury** <term>sublimate|original: *sublimivest*; the process of heating a substance to a vapor so it collects as a purified solid upon cooling.</term>. Afterward, place pure bodies into the pure **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurium*</note>; then grind it, boil it, and repeat it, and do not let yourself be vexed by doing such things.

**Ascanius** <note>A figure frequently appearing in the *Turba Philosophorum*, an ancient alchemical dialogue.</note>: Stir up a war between the ore and the **quicksilver** <term>quicksilver|original: *Quecksilber*; another name for mercury.</term>, until they perish and are ruined; when the ore has received the **quicksilver**, it makes it hard; but when the **living silver** <term>living silver|original: *argentum vivum*; the literal Latin translation of quicksilver or mercury.</term> receives the ore, then it is **congealed** <term>congealed|original: *congeliret*; the process of turning a liquid or gas into a solid mass.</term>. After that, the body of each is torn apart, and it does not become a...

--- Page 172 ---
<meta>This page continues from page 157, completing the discussion on the preparation of the alchemical "powder" through moistening and boiling. It transitions into the symbolic union of the "Red Man" and "Wife" to produce the volatile spirit known as Ethelia, citing authorities like Gratianus and Calid to validate the "philosophy" of the ancients.</meta>

powder only through a diligent and frequent moistening and boiling. But place the **red man** with the **wife**, until husband and wife become one *ethelia* <term>ethelia|original: *Ethelia*; a technical term often used in Greek and Arabic alchemy to describe a refined, volatile spirit or "spiritualized" mercury.</term>. For whoever converts these into a *spirit* <term>spirit|original: *Spiritum*; in alchemy, a volatile substance capable of rising during distillation.</term> through *ethelia*—that is, quicksilver—and afterwards makes it red, he can tinge <term>tinge|original: *tingiren*; to alchemically transform or "dye" a base metal into gold or silver.</term> all bodies.

Now, what kind of ore that may be, *Gratianus* <note>Likely referring to a figure from the *Turba Philosophorum*, an influential early alchemical assembly text.</note> teaches with these words: 

> therefore make the *laton* <term>laton|original: *Latonem*; an impure metallic body, often compared to brass, that must be purified or "whitened."</term> white, that is, ore with the *mercury* <term>mercury|original: *Mercurio*.</term>. For the *laton* is a composite *body* <term>body|original: *Corpus*.</term> of sun and moon <note>original: "Sonn und Mond"; in alchemical symbolism, Sun refers to Gold and Moon refers to Silver.</note>, imperfect and *citrine* <term>citrine|original: *Citrin*; a pale lemon-yellow or golden color.</term> in color.

We have now done enough and more than enough for our purpose, and proven that our *philosophy* <term>philosophy|original: *Philosophia*; here meaning the Alchemical Art or "The Hermetic Philosophy."</term>, which we have attained with manifold effort and many sleepless nights, is the teaching and right truth of the ancients.

Therefore, whoever deviates from this goal will either deceive others or be deceived by others (with the sole and singular exception of the work of *Saturn* <note>Saturn is the alchemical symbol for Lead; the "Work of Saturn" often refers to a specific process of purifying lead or using it as a starting point for the Philosopher's Stone.</note>, which must be made through the *subtilization* of the *principles* <term>principles|original: *principiorum*; the fundamental elements or "seeds" of a substance.</term>.)

Therefore, follow my teaching, which is certain and indeed proven by experience. For as *Calid* <note>Khalid ibn Yazid (died c. 704), an Umayyad prince and one of the earliest figures associated with the translation of alchemy into Arabic.</note> speaks in Chapter 3: 

> you should know that their medicines are related to the natures, as the *philosophers* have said in their books: that one nature approaches the other, and one nature makes itself like the other, and that one nature is sunk into the other, also that one nature makes the other white and also red: that also one birth is maintained with the other, and also one generation surpasses the other.

<summary>This page describes the symbolic marriage of substances to create "Ethelia" (mercury) and the whitening of the "Laton" (impure metal). The author asserts that this method aligns with the ancient truths of philosophers like Gratianus and Calid.</summary>

<keywords>Ethelia, Quicksilver, Laton, Sun and Moon, Alchemical Marriage, Gratianus, Calid, Work of Saturn, Tinging</keywords>

--- Page 173 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the chemical and symbolic preparation of the "Stone." It moves from the union of the "Red Man" and "Wife" mentioned on the previous page to the stages of conception, pregnancy, and the eventual "birth" of the alchemical King. It emphasizes the necessity of dissolution and patience in the process.</meta>

Therefore, just as it is written in the *Path of Paths* <note>original: "Semita Semitæ"; a medieval alchemical treatise often attributed to the scholar Albertus Magnus.</note>; 
when the earth retains a little of the *quicksilver* <term>quicksilver|original: *argentum vivum*; liquid mercury, considered a primary "female" or "watery" principle in alchemy.</term> within itself, it is called a "conception," 
and [this occurs] when the husband is at work with the wife—that is, the *quicksilver* with the earth. 
And this is what the *philosophers* <note>The "Philosophers" refers to the revered ancient and medieval masters of the Hermetic Art.</note> have said: that 
our *Great Work* <term>Great Work|original: *Magisterium*; the complete process of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</term> is nothing other than man and wife 
and their joining together. Therefore, when the water is added—that is, the 
*quicksilver*—it grows in the earth and is increased, 
for the earth becomes white, and then it is called the "impregnation." 
Afterward, the *ferment* <term>ferment|original: *Fermentum*; a substance added to the alchemical "body" to act like leaven in bread, triggering a transformation.</term> is *congealed* <term>congealed|original: *coaguliret*; the process of a liquid becoming a solid or semi-solid state.</term>; 
that is, it is joined with the perfected *body* <term>body|original: *Cörper*; the solid metallic matter.</term> 
which has been prepared, as stated, until it becomes one in color and 
appearance. At that point, it is called *Birth* <note>original: "Ortus"; Latin for rising, beginning, or birth.</note>. 
For then our Stone is born, which is called a "king" 
by the philosophers. Of which it is said in the *Art of Chemistry* <note>original: "Arte Chymica"; a general reference to alchemical literature.</note>: 
if someone were to burn that same flower and 
separate the *elements* <term>elements|original: *Elementa*; the four basic building blocks of nature—earth, air, fire, and water—which alchemists sought to balance.</term> from one another, 
the generative seed would perish, and no creature could 
join them together again so that they might produce anything.

Therefore, I conclude with *Avicenna* <note>Ibn Sina (c. 980–1037), a Persian physician and philosopher whose views on the "impossibility" of transmutation were a major point of debate for later European alchemists.</note> that the 
true beginning of our work is the *dissolution* <term>dissolution|original: *Dissolutio*; the breakdown of a solid substance into a liquid form.</term> of the Stone; 
for the dissolved bodies are made of a spiritual nature—
that is, because they are more dry—for the *solution* <term>solution|original: *Solutio*.</term> of 
the body happens through the *congealing* <term>congealing|original: *Congelation*; the transition of a spirit or liquid into a fixed, solid form.</term> of the spirit.

Therefore, be patient; boil, grind, and make it yellow 
like wax, and do not let it vex you to 
repeat this many times; for whatever is soaked or moistened 
is made soft by the water. The more you

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process using the metaphor of human reproduction (conception, pregnancy, and birth) to explain the union of quicksilver and "earth." It concludes by citing Avicenna on the importance of dissolving the "body" to spiritualize the matter.</summary>
<keywords>Semita Semitæ, quicksilver, Great Work, fermentation, birth of the Stone, the King, Avicenna, dissolution, coagulation</keywords>

--- Page 174 ---
<meta>This page concludes the alchemical treatise by Edward Kelley, following the discussion of the "Birth" of the philosophical stone. It details the mechanical and thermal processes required to refine the substance and lists the three essential stages of dissolution.</meta>

it will be rubbed; the more you rub, the more you will soften it and 
make the coarse parts subtle <note>In alchemy, "subtle" refers to making a substance fine, volatile, or purified, as opposed to "gross" or "coarse" matter.</note> until it is enough. And these parts 
are divided when the spirits are impasted <term>impasted|original: *impastiret*; the process of mixing a dry powder with a liquid to form a dough-like consistency.</term>, 
and everything that is impasted is thoroughly 
dissolved; and this impastation occurs through excessive 
grinding, yellowing, and roasting. For through the 
tearing, roasting, and through the fire, the bound 
parts and the slimy parts that are within the bodies are divided.

Lastly, you Sons of Wisdom should know, as 
the writer of *The Chemical Art* <note>original: *Artis Chymicæ*; likely referring to a compendium of alchemical writings.</note> says, 
that there are three solutions <note>original: *Solutiones*; the process of turning a solid into a liquid state.</note> 
in the Philosophical Work: the first is of the raw 
body; the second is of the Philosophical Earth; the third 
is placed in the augmentation <term>augmentation|original: *augmentation*; the process of increasing the power or quantity of the alchemical medicine.</term> or multiplication.

When this has been well-weighed and considered with 
diligence, and if diligence is used alongside it, then the 
Mastery <term>Mastery|original: *Magisterium*; the completion of the Great Work of alchemy.</term> will become well known to you—
for the sake of which I have suffered my great misfortune, 
and how much misery I have endured, *History* 
will reveal more than enough in the time to come. <note>Edward Kelley (1555–1597) was famously imprisoned by Emperor Rudolf II when he failed to produce the gold he had promised; he likely died following an escape attempt.</note>

### End of Edward Kelley’s Treatise.
